# NOTES.md -- part ccc22 (Criminal Code (Canada), R.S.C., 1985, c. C-46, lines 12463-13305: ss 573 to 672)

Part XIX.1 (Nunavut Court of Justice) and Part XX (procedure in jury trials and general provisions).
Written by part agent `ccc22` (Sonnet 5.5), no subagents, resumed on 2026-11-16 after the account usage-limit stop of about 2026-11-12T21:20Z.
Generated by `work/notes_gen.py` from the deposited `.l4` files, the seat specification and the prose in `work/notes_text.py`; the generator is not shipped.

**About this text.**
The English text of the Criminal Code from the Justice Laws Website consolidation (current to 2026-09-30), under the Reproduction Order and the Open Government Licence - Canada (`inputs/STUB-SOURCE-LICENSE.md`).
It reached this part as a plain-text rendering made by a converter script; it is not the official text, and nothing here is claimed verified against the official text.
The French version is equally authoritative and is not among the sources.

**How the claims below were checked.**
Run by script on 2026-11-16: `check.sh` on both stable builds (the numbers are printed at the end of this file), `srcrefs.py check --refs` and `--strict` over every `ccc22-*.l4` file, `work/lint.py` over the same files (every `JUST FALSE` and `NOTHING` result carries its `FALSE-kind` or `NOTHING-kind` comment with a quotation found in the text; every top-level name, type and constructor begins with its section label, or with "Under section" in the seat files).
Expected values in the tests are written by hand from the text beside each assertion; none was copied from the output of the code.
Nothing here has been read by a lawyer.

**Files.**
Layer 1: `ccc22-nunavut`, `-indictments`, `-counts`, `-joinder`, `-at-large`, `-amendment`, `-pleas`, `-organizations`, `-juries`, `-trial`, `-evidence`, `-verdicts`, `-jury-defects` (`.l4`), each with a `ccc22-tests-<same>.l4`.
Layer 2: `ccc22-seat.l4` and `ccc22-cases-seat.l4`.
Also `NOTES.md`, `PROGRESS.md`; `NEEDS-CORE.md` is not needed (no constructor of the core was missing: the sheriff, an officer of the court and a judge of the Court of Appeal of Nunavut are not `Party` constructors, and no regulative rule names them; they are `BOOLEAN` facts).

## Coverage (ccc22)

Built from the text by `work/units.py`: one row per section, subsection, paragraph and subparagraph of lines 12463 to 13305, so that every one of the 815 non-blank lines of the range lies in exactly one row (the script reports 0 lines missing).
The range holds no schedule or table; its two section-level definitions are "Agreement" in 579.001(5) (`s 579.001(5) the instrument is the Agreement`) and "court" in 601(10) (the type `s 601(10) the body`), and each has its row.
A Section 2 term used here is an input named for its section (see Seams).
Rows: 562. Dispositions: encoded: 227, fork: 263, inert: 61, repealed or blank in this text: 11.
Layer 1 holds 367 functions in 423 cited blocks (`@ref`), and every function has at least one assertion in its tests file.
A row is `encoded` when a layer-1 rule or type cites its lines with `@ref` and no fork line attaches to it, and `fork (F-n)` when the rule that encodes it carries a fork line in the register below.
`inert (reason 1)` rows hold only a heading or a marginal note.
Before this pass the table had no row for the lead-in lines of 650.01(3), 657.3(3), 658(3) and 667(1) and for paragraph 601(3)(c): their `@ref` ranges stopped one line short, and have been extended so that those lines are quoted and cited (audit: rows before 562, after 562; uncited lines before 5 rows, after 0 rows).

## Fork register (ccc22)

Every site where the text can be read two or more ways with different outcomes has a row.
The kinds below follow the rulings of the Guide: the rule of 2026-10-29 (assumed, not ruled as a whole), the ruling on periods, "where" and scope limits of 2026-11-05 (a period the text fixes in its own words: outside it `JUST FALSE`; a "where" condition that fails: `JUST FALSE`; a scope limit with no "only": `NOTHING`), the ruling of 2026-11-05 that "when", "upon" and "whenever" count as "where", the ruling of 2026-11-05 that six questions are decided case by case (an "unless" or "except" once met; a bare "must" not done; "listed in Schedule N"; a duty whose "if" or "where" is unmet; "(other than X)"; a missing event in the ledger), and the ruling of 2026-11-12 on functions named for a grant or a power.
Each row names the function, the answer taken, the other reading, and the words that license the reading taken.
**Nothing below is decided for a lawyer.**

Counts by kind: a "may" function, failed "if" gate: 37; a "may" function, "where"/"when"/period gate: 46; an "entitled" function: 7; a duty whose own condition is unmet: 58; an "unless"/"except" once met: 33; "for greater certainty" clause: 4.

### A -- a "may" function, failed "if" gate (37)

### B -- a "may" function, "where"/"when"/period gate (46)

Ruling (b) of the periods ruling of 2026-11-05 says `JUST FALSE` for a failed "where"; ruling (c) of the 2026-11-05 delegated ruling makes "when", "upon" and "whenever" count as "where"; ruling (a) says `JUST FALSE` outside a period the text fixes in its own words.
The Guide's line that a grant limited in its own wording gives `NOTHING` points the other way, so this group is **assumed, not ruled** (the sweep of 2026-11-12 lists it for the maintainer or a lawyer).
The equivalents "so long as", "in the case of", "in the absence of", "after ..." and "before ..." are taken with this group, **assumed, not ruled**.

| id | function | answer taken | other reading | the text that licenses the reading taken |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F-8 | `s 574(1.1) the prosecutor may prefer an indictment where no preliminary inquiry was requested` | JUST FALSE when the "where"/"when"/period gate fails | NOTHING (a grant limited by its own wording, case outside the limit) | "at any time after the person has made an election, re-election or deemed election" |
| F-13 | `s 578(1) the court may issue a summons or a warrant for the arrest of the accused` | JUST FALSE when the "where"/"when"/period gate fails | NOTHING (a grant limited by its own wording, case outside the limit) | "Where notice of the recommencement of proceedings has been given pursuant to subsection 579(2) or an indictment has been filed with the court" |

### F -- "for greater certainty" clause (4)

A clause that begins "For greater certainty" clarifies what another provision already gives; it says nothing about a case outside its own facts.
Subsection 662(6) carries no such words and answers `JUST FALSE` outside its facts (a "where" gate).

| id | function | answer taken | other reading | the text that licenses the reading taken |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| F-167 | `s 662(5) the accused may be convicted of an offence under section 320.13` | NOTHING outside the clause's facts | JUST FALSE (the clause read as closing the list) | "For greater certainty, when a count charges an offence under section 220, 221 or 236 arising out of the operation of a conveyance, and the evidence..." |
| F-168 | `s 662(5.1) the accused may be found guilty of the offence that is proved` | NOTHING outside the clause's facts | JUST FALSE (the clause read as closing the list) | "For greater certainty, if a count charges an offence under subsection 263.1(1) and the evidence does not prove that offence but proves an included..." |
| F-170 | `s 662(7) the accused may be found guilty of the offence that is proved` | NOTHING outside the clause's facts | JUST FALSE (the clause read as closing the list) | "For greater certainty, if a count charges an offence under subsection 320.1001(1) and the evidence does not prove that offence but proves an includ..." |

### Other forks of this part

Forks that are not a repeated pattern, one line each (the provision, the function, the answer taken, the other reading).

- **s 579(2), the period "within one year after the entry of the stay".** The function `s 579(2) the proceedings are deemed never to have been commenced` counts a notice given on the anniversary itself as in time; section 579.001(4) says "on or before the first anniversary" in terms (text line 12576), and 579(2) is read the same way. Other reading: the anniversary is outside "within one year after", so a notice on that day is late. Tests: the pair of assertions on 10 March 2026 (in time) and 11 March 2026 (one day late).
- **s 579(2), "whichever is the earlier".** The last day for the notice is the earlier of the first anniversary and the day the time within which the proceedings could have been commenced expires; the second day is another provision's and is an input. Other reading: none seen; the words are unambiguous, but the expiry day is not stated by this part.
- **s 640(1), a challenge made and the judge not satisfied.** The text says the judge shall determine whether the ground is true and, if satisfied, the juror shall not be sworn. The function `s 640(1) the judge must determine whether the ground is true and, if satisfied that it is, the juror shall not be sworn` answers `JUST TRUE` for "challenge made and the judge satisfied", `JUST FALSE` for "no challenge" and `NOTHING` for "challenge made, judge not satisfied" (the subsection is silent about the juror then). Other reading: `JUST TRUE` for the duty to determine whenever a challenge is made. The seat question asks only the second limb (see the audit note).
- **s 645(2) and s 645(4), "from time to time in the same sittings" and "in any case tried without a jury".** Read as scope limits (ruling (c) of 2026-11-05): outside them the answers are `NOTHING`. Other reading: `JUST FALSE`, if "in the same sittings" is a period the text fixes.
- **s 577, "even if the accused has not been given the opportunity to request a preliminary inquiry, a preliminary inquiry has been commenced but not concluded or a preliminary inquiry has been held and the accused has been discharged".** The function takes the person's position in the preliminary-inquiry process as a choice of four (the three situations the text names and "none of those situations") and treats "none of those situations" as outside the section (`NOTHING`). Other reading: `JUST FALSE`, because the section then gives no route to an indictment.
- **Stated periods of grants (this pass).** The first pass answered `NOTHING` outside a period that a grant states in its own words ("at any time before the jury retires", "at any time after the jury has been sworn and before it gives its verdict" and the like), following the brief's line on "a stated period". On 2026-11-16 this part changed these eight to `JUST FALSE` outside the period, as its own decision under ruling (a) of 2026-11-05 and against BRIEF.md line 60; the name-test sweep did not ask for it (see the sweep subsection below, which lists the eight and how to revert). Other reading: `NOTHING`.

### Name-test sweep of 2026-11-12

The three tests of the sweep were applied to every layer-1 function whose name contains "may", "permitted", "power", "entitled" or "authorised/authorized" (154 functions by name).
A function can appear in more than one column when its branches fall in more than one (for example a grant with an "if" gate that gives `NOTHING` and a "where" gate that gives `JUST FALSE`); the counts below are of functions per column.

| flipped to NOTHING | kept JUST FALSE (gate closed by the text) | listed: "may" with where/when/upon | listed: "entitled" |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 37 | 22 | 46 | 8 |

**The first column lists functions that answer `NOTHING` where an "if" gate fails; this pass flipped none of them.** The instruction flips a grant only when it answers `JUST FALSE` because an "if" gate failed, and every assertion restated would carry `-- RECONCILED 2026-11-12`; the only layer-1 restatements go the other way, from `NOTHING` to `JUST FALSE` (below), so no function was moved from `JUST FALSE` to `NOTHING` by this pass. The 37 are listed with their fork rows (kind A) so the reader can see which grants a failed "if" leaves unanswered.

Outside the sweep: named "it is lawful": **it is lawful, scoped: 0; it is lawful, unscoped and retitled: 0** (no function of this part has such a name; "unlawfully" occurs only inside the name of `s 656 the person is presumed to have stolen or unlawfully possessed the valuable mineral`, which is a presumption, not a justification).
The remaining 50 functions whose names match have no `JUST FALSE` branch at all (a function with one is in a column above), and are not in the four columns.

**A decision of this part, not an instruction of the sweep.** The sweep instruction says to list the "where"-kind grants in the fork register and not to flip them; it moves no answer from `NOTHING` to `JUST FALSE`. This part nevertheless changed eight grants whose gate is a period the text fixes in its own words, so that outside the period they answer `JUST FALSE`, under ruling (a) of 2026-11-05 (BRIEF.md line 115) and against BRIEF.md line 60, whose list of `NOTHING` cases names `when` and `a stated period` among the limits on a grant: `s 574(1.1)`, `s 579(1)`, `s 591(4)`, `s 631(2.2)`, `s 632`, `s 647(1)`, `s 650.1` and `s 652(1)`. Ten assertions of those eight, in four tests files (`-joinder` 1, `-indictments` 3, `-juries` 2, `-trial` 4), were restated in place, each with `-- RECONCILED 2026-11-12: was ...`, and each of the ten had expected `NOTHING`. **Assumed, not ruled.** The other reading is `NOTHING`, and a sibling part that follows line 60 will answer these cases `NOTHING`. **To revert:** change the period branch of each of the eight functions back to `NOTHING` with a `NOTHING-kind (limited by its own wording)` comment, and restore the ten assertions to the text after `was` in their RECONCILED comments.
Callers (checked by grep over the layer-1 and seat files on 2026-11-16): only one of the eight is called by another function. `s 579(1) the proceedings are stayed and any undertaking or release order relating to them is vacated` takes the answer of `s 579(1) the Attorney General may direct the clerk to enter a stay` as a `MAYBE BOOLEAN` parameter and answers `JUST TRUE` only when that answer is `JUST TRUE`, otherwise `NOTHING` (once the entry is made), so the change of that answer from `NOTHING` to `JUST FALSE` cannot alter its result. The seat question for 579(1) builds the same call. The other seven (574(1.1), 591(4), 631(2.2), 632, 647(1), 650.1 and 652(1)) are called by nothing but their own tests, and the seat layer has no question for any of them: each is a power of an official, not something asked from the seat of the accused.

#### Kept JUST FALSE: the text closes the gate itself, or the function is a bar, a duty or the provision's own question (22)

The name test matches by wording alone, so four of these are not grants: `s 601(3)` and `s 669.2(2)` are duties ("must"), `s 635(2)` is the order in which the defence challenges, and `s 672` says what the Act leaves unaltered. They are kept as they answer, and listed here because they match.

- `s 573.1 an application for review may be made, subject to subsection (2)`: flat negative ("may not")
- `s 573.1(3) the judge of the Court of Appeal of Nunavut may grant relief under subsection (4)`: "only if"

## Jural relations (ccc22)

One row per rule of layer 1.
The holder and the bearer are assigned from each rule's wording by the generator (`work/notes_gen.py`): the party the text names as the actor is the holder of a power or privilege or the bearer of a duty; where the text names no actor the row says what the rule treats as the other side.
Hohfeld's correlatives are used: a claim (right) against a duty; a privilege (liberty) against a no-right; a power against a liability; an immunity against a disability.
**This table is assumed, not ruled, and has not been read by a lawyer.**
A rule that creates no relation (a definition, a deeming, an effect, a helper) is listed once as `none`.
The wording of each kind comes from the verb in the rule's name ("must" and "shall" give a duty; "may" gives a power for an official and a privilege for the accused, counsel or a juror; "entitled" gives a claim; "bars" and "does not" give an immunity), so a rule whose name hides its verb may be mis-sorted.

## Marginal readings for a lawyer (ccc22)

Two readings are for a lawyer, not for this part to decide: (a) a failed "if" counts as the text's no, clause by clause, within the provision that holds the "if" (an inference: "if A then B" does not itself say "if not A then not B"), and flat negatives count as the text saying no; (b) a prohibition or grant limited by its own wording does not.
The status quo is taken for both; each branch that rests on one is listed with the other reading.
The rows for reading (b) are the branches where a prohibition or grant limited by its own wording is answered `NOTHING` and tagged `[M-b]` in the code; the many other `NOTHING` branches are the rule of 2026-10-29 as the Guide gives it ("silent", "list not closed", "limited by its own wording" and so on) and are not repeated here.

Branches resting on reading (a): 237. Branches resting on reading (b): 3. Functions affected: 202.

### Reading a result: FALSE and NOTHING (ccc22)

**A "no" in this part** is `JUST FALSE`, and it is given only where the text itself says no to the case in its own words: a "means" definition; a condition the text introduces with "if", "only if", "as long as", "so long as" or "unless" (and "while", "until", "except"); a list closed with "only" or "no others"; a flat negative ("does not", "shall not", "may not", "is not entitled"); a "where" condition that fails; a period the text fixes in its own words, outside the period.
For a duty ("shall"), `JUST FALSE` means the section puts no duty on these facts; for a bar it means the bar does not apply; for a grant it means the grant is not given on these facts.
It never means that the act is forbidden.

**"Not covered" is `NOTHING`.**
It is given where the text lists without closing the list, leaves a term undefined, is silent, points to another provision's or another Act's answer (those are inputs), or limits a grant or prohibition in its own wording and the case falls outside the limit and the limit is an "if" gate or a scope phrase.
A caller must never turn `NOTHING` into `FALSE`.
Where a bar names the thing the person may do ("without the consent of the accused"), a case that has the complement is `NOTHING`, not a grant.

**In the seat layer** a question is titled as the function answers it: "does the section bar ..." where the function is the bar, "may ..." where it is the permission, "must ..." where it is the duty, "am I entitled to ..." where it is the claim.
`JUST TRUE` is then "yes" to the title as asked, `JUST FALSE` is "no, on these facts", and `NOTHING` is "the text does not say".

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| s 606(4.1) | ccc28 s 752 | `s 752 the offence is a serious personal injury offence` | `MAYBE BOOLEAN` | `ccc22-pleas` |

| ss 605, 620 to 625, 650(1.1) | keel s 2 | `s 2 superior court of criminal jurisdiction: the judge sits in one`, `s 2 court of criminal jurisdiction: the court is one`, `s 2 organization: the accused is one`, `s 2 property: the thing is real or personal property` | `MAYBE BOOLEAN` | `ccc22-amendment`, `ccc22-organizations`, `ccc22-trial`, `ccc22-counts` |

Seam this part answers for another part: `s 577 a direct indictment has been preferred` (`ccc22-indictments`), `MAYBE BOOLEAN`, asked by ccc21 for ss 536, 536.1, 561 and 561.1.
The function takes the plain facts of section 577 (an indictment has been preferred; the person's position in the preliminary-inquiry process; the Attorney General's part in the prosecution; the personal consent; a judge's order) and answers `JUST TRUE` for (a) and (b) when the condition holds, `JUST FALSE` when the condition fails, and `NOTHING` outside the section.

A seam the plan lacks, or takes differently: sections 573.1 and 573.2 are in the plan's list as asking ccc21's ss 536, 548 and 552, but this part does not take ccc21's answers there.
Section 573.1(2) and section 573.2(1) turn on "a decision or order of a kind that could only be made ... by a superior court of criminal jurisdiction or a judge as defined in section 552", and the code asks that as a plain `BOOLEAN` fact (`the decision or order is of a kind that could only be made in a province or a territory other than Nunavut by a superior court of criminal jurisdiction or a judge as defined in section 552`), so ccc21's section 552 is not an input of this part.
The part also reads section 2 terms that the plan lists (`indictment`, `count`, `prosecutor`, `justice`, `counsel`, `Attorney General`, `clerk of the court`, `provincial court judge`) as ordinary facts of each rule rather than as inputs where the rule only mentions the word, and as `MAYBE BOOLEAN` inputs only where the rule turns on the definition (the four rows above).

## Seat layer (ccc22)

Layer 2 is `ccc22-seat.l4` with its cases in `ccc22-cases-seat.l4`: 104 questions asked from the seat of a person who is, or may be, accused of an offence under the Criminal Code, about the procedure of their own trial from the indictment to the verdict and the proof of previous convictions.
**Assumed, not ruled:** Singapore's Penal Code is the model (the maintainer's instruction of 2026-11-09, per the brief); this is a plain seat layer with no charge-generator contract.
Each question is named "Under section N, ..." with the Act's own numbering, carries `@export` and an `@desc` on every input worded as the accused would be asked, takes plain inputs (yes or no, a number, a date, or a choice from the owning module's list), and calls the layer-1 rule of its section with those inputs; no rule is restated.
Where a layer-1 rule takes the answer of another rule as a `MAYBE` value (ss 579(1), 579(2) and 657.3(3)(c)), the question asks the plain facts and builds the value inside its body.
Questions are grouped under `§` headings in the text's order; the first group only arises for a case before the Nunavut Court of Justice, and says so in its heading comment so that a form can ask "Is your case in Nunavut?" first.
Provisions that address only an official (the Attorney General's direction, the clerk's entry, the sheriff's appointment, the judge's own powers) are encoded in layer 1 for what they decide, and are in layer 2 only where they mean something to the accused; there is no seat for an official.
Every question that takes an input has at least two cases, written by hand from the text; the two that take no input are the class of offence of 648(2) (one assertion) and its punishment (three assertions, one for each field the subsection states).

### Seat-question audit, second pass of 2026-11-12 (ccc22)

Every seat question was read again against the BODY of the layer-1 function it calls, not against its own title: its name and `@export` text, its inputs and their `@desc`, and the branches of the function (which input gives `JUST TRUE`, which `JUST FALSE`, which `NOTHING`).
Retitled only where the title asked a different limb, route or scope than the body answers, carried a premise or consequence the function does not compute, had the wrong polarity or modal, or asked a different type of answer.
**Seat questions read: 104. Retitled: 1. Read and left: 103.**

Retitled:

- `Under section 640(1), must the judge determine whether the ground is true and, if satisfied that it is, leave the juror unsworn` was retitled to `Under section 640(1), must the judge leave a challenged juror unsworn`. Reason: the title asked two limbs (the duty to determine, and the juror left unsworn) but the body answers the second only: a challenge made and the judge satisfied is JUST TRUE, no challenge is JUST FALSE, and a challenge made with the judge not satisfied is NOTHING, although the duty to determine is then still owed. The two assertions of the question were restated in place with `-- RECONCILED 2026-11-12`.

Direction check (the brief asks for every question whose name and body differ in direction or in subject to be listed and fixed): after the retitling none remains. The questions titled as a bar are the 16 that call a "bars" function (573.1(2), 574(3), 576(3), 582, 589, 598(1), 603 postponement, 607(6), 612(1), 648(1), 657.1(3), 657.3(6), 664, 667(4), 670, 671); for each, `JUST TRUE` means the bar applies and `JUST FALSE` that it does not, as the function answers.

Read and left, with the reason, for the questions where the comparison needed a second look:

- `Under section 578(1), may the court issue a summons or a warrant for my arrest`: the body takes the document as an input and answers for the one named (a summons or a warrant: `JUST TRUE`; any other document: `NOTHING`), which is what the function's own name says.
- `Under section 579(1), are the proceedings against me stayed and any undertaking or release order relating to them vacated`: the body combines whether the direction was authorized (the "may" function) with whether the entry was made, and both are computed from the plain inputs.
- `Under section 601(1), may the objection be taken`: the function answers `JUST FALSE` where the text says the objection "shall be taken by motion ... before the accused enters a plea, and thereafter only by leave", which is the title's question ("may it be taken") answered no; the duty wording is the text's own.
- `Under section 626(1), is the person qualified to serve as a juror in criminal proceedings in the province`: the function relays the province's own answer as plain inputs and answers `NOTHING` otherwise; the title restates the function's name.
- `Under section 643(3), does the failure to comply affect the validity of a proceeding`: the only answers are `JUST FALSE` ("does not affect") and `NOTHING`; the title restates the function's name and the direction agrees.
- `Under section 648(2), is the person guilty of an offence punishable on summary conviction`: the title restates the function's name; the input is whether the person failed to comply with 648(1).
- `Under section 650(1), does the accused’s presence meet the requirement`: the function answers whether presence in person or by an authorized audioconference or videoconference meets the requirement; same direction.
- `Under section 653(1), may the judge take the step`: the step is asked as an input with its list in the `@desc`; the title restates the function's name.
- `Under section 657.3(3)(c), have the accused or their counsel provided the material not later than the close of the case for the prosecution`: the body answers the duty as a status ("have ... provided"), with the dates as inputs; a "must" title would ask a different question than the three-valued status the function returns.
- `Under section 662(3), may the jury on that count find me guilty of any other offence`: the function is the flat negative ("shall not ... find the accused guilty of any other offence"); `JUST FALSE` is "no", the title's direction.
- `Under section 662(2), may the jury find me not guilty of first degree murder but guilty of second degree murder or an attempt to commit second degree murder`: the function answers `JUST TRUE` for exactly those two findings and `NOTHING` for the rest ("for greater certainty"); same direction and same limb.

The other 92 read-and-left questions restate the function's own name, in the direction its body answers, over the same limb.


## Findings: what the text turns out to say (ccc22)

1. **Section 577 gives the seam answer ccc21 asks for.** `s 577 a direct indictment has been preferred` answers `JUST TRUE` when an indictment has been preferred in one of the three situations the section names (the accused has not been given the opportunity to request a preliminary inquiry; a preliminary inquiry has been commenced but not concluded; a preliminary inquiry has been held and the accused discharged) and the route of paragraph (a) (a prosecution conducted or joined by the Attorney General, with the personal consent in writing of the Attorney General or Deputy Attorney General filed in court) or of paragraph (b) (any other case, a judge so orders) is met. If the route is not met the answer is `JUST FALSE`; it is `NOTHING` where no indictment has been preferred and where none of the three situations holds (an ordinary indictment).
2. **Notice of recommencement of a stay.** Section 579(2) says "within one year after the entry of the stay of proceedings, or before the expiration of the time within which the proceedings could have been commenced, whichever is the earlier"; section 579.001(4) says "on or before the first anniversary" in terms. The two are read alike (see the fork line).
3. **Limited lists of special pleas.** Section 606(1) closes its list with "and no others", so any other plea is `JUST FALSE`; section 607(1) lists four special pleas (autrefois acquit, autrefois convict, pardon, an expungement order) without "only", so a plea of justification (section 611) is `NOTHING` there and `JUST TRUE` under 611(1) when the accused is charged with publishing a defamatory libel and pleads its truth and public benefit.
4. **Section 643(3) names only itself and sections 631, 635 and 641.** A failure to comply with the directions of another section (for example 636 or 642) is not decided there, so it answers `NOTHING`.
5. **"For greater certainty".** Subsections 662(2), 662(5), 662(5.1) and 662(7) begin with those words and answer `NOTHING` outside their own facts; subsection 662(6) has no such words and answers `JUST FALSE` outside its facts (a "where" gate).
6. **Absence from the trial.** Section 650(1) requires the accused to be present in court during the whole of the trial, in person or by an authorized audioconference or videoconference; 650(1.1) allows counsel in the accused's place for a part with both parties' consent except a part in which a witness's evidence is taken; 650.01(3)(a) lets designated counsel appear without the accused for any part other than oral evidence of a witness, the selection of jurors and an application for habeas corpus. The two provisions carve out the witness's evidence in the same way and are separate rules.
7. **Previous convictions.** Section 664 bars any reference to previous convictions in an indictment for an offence whose punishment is greater because of them; section 666 lets the prosecutor adduce evidence of a previous conviction only "in answer" to evidence of the accused's good character and before a verdict is returned. A verdict already returned gives `JUST FALSE`; a case where the prosecutor does not adduce it in answer gives `NOTHING`.
8. **Certificates.** Section 667(1)(a) makes a certificate evidence of the conviction if it is signed by one of three named persons and the accused is proved to be the offender; any other signer gives `JUST FALSE`; 667(3) lets the accused require the signer's attendance only with leave of the court; 667(4) bars receiving a certificate of the two kinds without reasonable notice and a copy.
9. **Time limits on grants are stated in the text's own words.** Sections 574(1.1), 579(1), 591(4), 631(2.2), 632, 647(1), 650.1 and 652(1) each state the period within which the grant may be used ("at any time after the person has made an election", "before judgment", "before or during the trial", "before the clerk of the court draws out the cards", "before the commencement of a trial", "before the jury retires", "before the charge to the jury", "after the jury has been sworn and before it gives its verdict"). Outside the period they answer `JUST FALSE`: this part's own decision under ruling (a) of 2026-11-05, against BRIEF.md line 60 (see the fork line and the sweep subsection).
10. **The grounds of review in section 573.1(3) are a table.** Paragraph (a) lists grounds for every kind of decision or order named in subsection (1); paragraphs (b), (c), (e) and (f) are for kinds (1)(a), (1)(b), (1)(e) and (1)(f), and (d) for (1)(c) or (1)(d). The helper `s 573.1(3) the ground is one the subsection names for that kind of decision or order` is tested directly in `ccc22-tests-nunavut.l4` (eight assertions added in this pass; it had been tested only through the function that calls it).
11. **Decisions about parties.** The sheriff, an officer of the court and a judge of the Court of Appeal of Nunavut are not `Party` constructors of the core, and no regulative rule names them; they are `BOOLEAN` facts in the decision functions. The regulative rules use the core's `a judge`, `a juror`, `counsel for the accused`, `the accused`, `the Attorney General`, `the clerk of the court`, `the court` and `the prosecutor`.
12. **Ten regulative rules are run with `#TRACE`**, each with a decision-function twin that is asserted: 579(2) (a `MAY`), 579.001(2), 606(2), 606(4.1), 606(4.3), 625.1(2), 631(2.1), 649(1) (a `SHANT`), 651(1) and 657.3(3)(c). The outcome of each trace is written beside it in the tests file, and the outcomes are the same on both stable builds (see check.sh below).

## What the stable binary does not accept (ccc22)

Each item was run on the stable build (1.6.110, `l4-main-110`) on 2026-11-16 unless it says otherwise.

- **`#ASSERT` cannot compare a `DEONTIC` value.** `#ASSERT <regulative rule> EQUALS FULFILLED` gives `eval/unsupported-equality`; `#ASSERT REFUSED <rule>` is a parse error (`parse/unexpected-directive`). Every regulative rule is therefore run with `#TRACE`, the outcome is written beside it, and the same duty is also a decision function that is asserted.
- **`LEST` after `MAY` is not reached.** A `MAY` rule whose time runs out unexercised traces as `FULFILLED` on the stable build; the same rule with `MUST` traces as `DEONTIC BREACHED ... BECAUSE "..."`. (The header of `check.sh` records that an unstable build lapses a `MAY` with a `LEST` to `BREACH`; `check.sh` does not see a different trace outcome, so the traces were compared on both builds by hand.)
- **A `SHANT` with no `WITHIN` leaves a residual obligation.** `PARTY a juror SHANT <action>` traced past any time prints `PARTY ... MUST NOT <action> HENCE FULFILLED`, not `FULFILLED`.
- **`x NOT EQUALS y` is a parse error** (`parse/unexpected-NOT`); write `NOT (x EQUALS y)`.
- **`IF ... ELSE IF ...` on the same indent is a parse error** (`parse/indentation`); every chain here is a `BRANCH`.
- **`WAIT UNTIL` is spelled `WAITUNTIL`** in a trace.
- **`python3 -I` ignores the working directory for imports**, so the helper scripts of `work/` insert their own folder into `sys.path`.
- Not a binary limit, and not asserted here: `NOT a AND b` should be written `(NOT a) AND b` (the brief's rule); the two spellings were not distinguished by a test in this part.

**Before and after.**
Before (`check.sh` run at the start of this resume, before any edit): 22 modules, 788 satisfied, 0 failed, 0 refused, 0 errors, the same on both builds.
After (the run above): 31 modules, 1492 satisfied, 0 failed, 0 refused, 0 errors, the same on both builds, exit 0.
The 704 more satisfied assertions are made of two things and nothing else, read from the per-module rows of the two logs: 696 are in nine files that did not exist at the baseline (`ccc22-evidence`, `ccc22-verdicts`, `ccc22-jury-defects` and the tests of `ccc22-trial`, `-evidence`, `-verdicts` and `-jury-defects`, written in the first part of this resume, and the two seat files, with 208 assertions in `ccc22-cases-seat`), and 8 are the direct tests added in `ccc22-tests-nunavut` (74 to 82) for the helper of section 573.1(3).
Every other file has the same count as before.
The restatements below change what an assertion expects, not how many there are.

**Assertions restated by the second pass** (each carries `-- RECONCILED 2026-11-12: was ...` with the reason): 12 in all.
In layer 1, ten: `ccc22-tests-joinder` 1 (591(4)), `ccc22-tests-indictments` 3 (574(1.1) and 579(1)), `ccc22-tests-juries` 2 (631(2.2) and 632), `ccc22-tests-trial` 4 (647(1), 650.1 and two for 652(1)); the four in `ccc22-tests-trial` are in a file that did not exist at the baseline, so they were restated against its own first draft.
In the seat cases, two: the cases of the question retitled for section 640(1).
**What the check does not see:** a different `#TRACE` outcome across the two builds.
That was compared by hand with `work/trace_cmp.py` on 2026-11-16: 17 traces in six tests files, the same result on both builds for all 17, and each result equal to the outcome written under it in the tests file.
Not run: anything on an unstable build; `JL4_LIBRARY_PATH` pinned; the Linux CI.
